Copy follows Joshua\'s teaching voice:\\r\\n// say what the thing is first, show it on one example, then let the student\\r\\n// do it. Modules 1 and 3 render the supplied Role introduction verbatim.\\r\\n\\r\\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- shared bits\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Panel({ children, tone = \\"ink\\" }: { children: React.ReactNode; tone?: \\"ink\\" | \\"yellow\\" }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div\\r\\n      className={cn(\\r\\n        \\"border-4 border-brutal-ink p-6 md:p-7 my-8 brutal-shadow\\",\\r\\n        tone === \\"ink\\" ? \\"bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper\\" : \\"bg-brutal-yellow text-brutal-ink\\",\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    >\\r\\n      <div className=\\"text-lg md:text-xl font-bold leading-snug\\">{children}</div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Label({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <span className=\\"block text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-brutal-ink-soft mb-2\\">\\r\\n      {children}\\r\\n    </span>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction AnswerChoice({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div className=\\"bg-white border-4 border-brutal-ink p-5 md:p-6 my-4 brutal-shadow-sm\\">\\r\\n      <Label>Answer choice</Label>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"text-lg md:text-xl font-semibold leading-snug\\">{children}</div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Verdict({ ok, children }: { ok: boolean; children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div\\r\\n      className={cn(\\r\\n        \\"mt-3 border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 flex gap-3 items-start\\",\\r\\n        ok ? \\"bg-brutal-green/40\\" : \\"bg-brutal-red/15\\",\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n      role=\\"status\\"\\r\\n    >\\r\\n      <span\\r\\n        className={cn(\\r\\n          \\"shrink-0 inline-flex items-center gap-1 border-2 border-brutal-ink px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-wider\\",\\r\\n          ok ? \\"bg-brutal-green\\" : \\"bg-brutal-red text-brutal-paper\\",\\r\\n        )}\\r\\n      >\\r\\n        {ok ? <Check className=\\"w-3.5 h-3.5\\" aria-hidden /> : <X className=\\"w-3.5 h-3.5\\" aria-hidden />}\\r\\n        {ok ? \\"Correct\\" : \\"Not quite\\"}\\r\\n      </span>\\r\\n      <span className=\\"text-base leading-relaxed\\">{children}</span>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction RightWrongCard({ ok, title, children }: { ok: boolean; title: string; children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div\\r\\n      className={cn(\\r\\n        \\"border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 flex gap-3 items-start\\",\\r\\n        ok ? \\"bg-brutal-green/30\\" : \\"bg-brutal-red/10\\",\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    >\\r\\n      <span\\r\\n        className={cn(\\r\\n          \\"shrink-0 inline-flex items-center gap-1 border-2 border-brutal-ink px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-wider\\",\\r\\n          ok ? \\"bg-brutal-green\\" : \\"bg-brutal-red text-brutal-paper\\",\\r\\n        )}\\r\\n      >\\r\\n        {ok ? <Check className=\\"w-3.5 h-3.5\\" aria-hidden /> : <X className=\\"w-3.5 h-3.5\\" aria-hidden />}\\r\\n        {ok ? \\"Right\\" : \\"Wrong\\"}\\r\\n      </span>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"text-base leading-relaxed\\">\\r\\n        <span className=\\"font-bold\\">{title}</span> {children}\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 1\\r\\n\\r\\nconst ANSWER_CHOICE_TRAPS_HTML = \\"<h1>Answer Choice Traps</h1>\\\\n\\\\n<section class=\\\\\\"section\\\\\\">\\\\n  <div class=\\\\\\"section-number\\\\\\">Answer Choice Type 1</div>\\\\n  <h2>Half right half wrong answers</h2>\\\\n\\\\n  <p><strong>An essential habit to have for the hardest LR questions (and RC questions) is to carefully parse the answer choices, looking for suspicious keywords or concepts and connections that seem out of place.</strong></p>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>The test makers love to offer us an answer choice that is only partially correct. The first half of the answer may be correct, but the second half will contain out of scope information, for instance. So it’s crucial to review each answer word by word, looking for red flags.</p>\\\\n</section>\\\\n\\\\n<section class=\\\\\\"section\\\\\\">\\\\n  <div class=\\\\\\"section-number\\\\\\">Answer Choice Type 2</div>\\\\n  <h2>Answers describing something that did not happen</h2>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>For questions whose correct answer describes a feature of the stimuli’s argument (Role, Method, Flaw), we need to keep a watchful eye for answer choices describing something that does not happen in the argument.</p>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>In fact, identifying answer choices that deviate from the stimulus is a sure-fire way to eliminate erroneous contenders from the selection. For these four types of questions, read the answer choice, think about what it means, <span class=\\\\\\"underline\\\\\\">isolate any suspicious words in them</span>, and ask yourself, “does the author do this in their argument?”</p>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>But of course, this depends on the question type we are working on. Similar to Out of Scope answer choices, answer choices describing something that does not occur in the stimulus can work as correct Strengthen/Weaken answers, among others.</p>\\\\n</section>\\\\n\\\\n<section class=\\\\\\"section\\\\\\">\\\\n  <div class=\\\\\\"section-number\\\\\\">Answer Choice Type 3</div>\\\\n  <h2>Answer choices that are too vague/abstract</h2>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>Finally, we have seen how the correct answer can be vaguely worded. We have even practiced the art of extrapolating what an answer is referring to by analyzing its keywords.</p>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>Extracting certain keywords from vague or abstract answer choices in order to understand what that answer choice is actually talking about is also an invaluable skill. Unfortunately, it’s one of the most under-developed skills and often overlooked when students pursue speed at the expense of accuracy.</p>\\\\n\\\\n  \\\\n\\\\n  <p class=\\\\\\"final\\\\\\">Lastly, a common trick employed by the authors of the test is to have an almost perfect answer with a glaring error, usually due to one single word placed right next to the correct answer, which is worded vaguely. This is a trap that we shall see over and over again throughout the book.</p>\\\\n</section>\\";\\r\\nconst SOURCE_NOTE_CLASSES = \\"space-y-5 leading-relaxed [&_p]:my-5 [&_h1]:text-3xl [&_h1]:font-black [&_h1]:mb-6 [&_h2]:text-xl [&_h2]:font-bold [&_h2]:mt-6 [&_.section]:mt-8 [&_.section-number]:font-bold [&_.underline]:underline [&_.final]:font-semibold [&_.section:last-child]:border-2 [&_.section:last-child]:border-brutal-ink [&_.section:last-child]:bg-brutal-yellow/30 [&_.section:last-child]:p-5\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function WhyHardNote() {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div>\\r\\n      <div className={SOURCE_NOTE_CLASSES} dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: ROLE_CHOICE_INTRO_HTML }} />\\r\\n      <div className={SOURCE_NOTE_CLASSES} dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: ANSWER_CHOICE_TRAPS_HTML }} />\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 2\\r\\n\\r\\nconst STATEMENT_WORDS: { term: string; meaning: string; note?: string }[] = [\\r\\n  { term: \\"Premise\\", meaning: \\"A reason the author gives to support a conclusion.\\" },\\r\\n  { term: \\"Main conclusion\\", meaning: \\"The final point the author wants you to accept.\\" },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    term: \\"Intermediate conclusion\\",\\r\\n    meaning: \\"A claim that gets support and then gives support to the main conclusion.\\",\\r\\n    note: \\"Also called a supported premise.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  { term: \\"Fact\\", meaning: \\"A statement about the world, taken as true.\\", note: \\"\\\\\\"Water boils at 100 degrees Celsius at sea level.\\\\\\"\\" },\\r\\n  { term: \\"Decision\\", meaning: \\"A choice someone made or will make.\\", note: \\"\\\\\\"I\'m taking the train instead of the bus.\\\\\\"\\" },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    term: \\"Principle (general principle)\\",\\r\\n    meaning: \\"A rule about what should or shouldn\'t be done that covers many cases.\\",\\r\\n    note: \\"\\\\\\"People who are tired every morning should go to bed earlier\\\\\\" is a principle. \\\\\\"John is tired every morning, so he should go to bed earlier\\\\\\" is not. It\'s about one person.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    term: \\"Generalization\\",\\r\\n    meaning: \\"A broad statement about what is.\\",\\r\\n    note: \\"\\\\\\"All swans are white.\\\\\\" A principle says what should be. A generalization says what is.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    term: \\"Hypothesis\\",\\r\\n    meaning: \\"A possible explanation: a proposed cause.\\",\\r\\n    note: \\"Not just any claim. A hypothesis is a guess about why something happened.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n];\\r\\n"}'}]

John’s unhappiness despite his income is the source example.\\",\\r\\n25:      \\"Does the statement illustrate the rule? Does the argument require that rule? John’s situation illustrates why money alone does not bring happiness.\\",\\r\\n30:    \\"breakdownHtml\\": \\"<p>Whenever we are faced with an answer choice that looks super abstract, the first thing to do is to always <strong>extract what keywords we can</strong> to try to make sense of the whole thing. Three words stand out in this answer choice: “illustrate”, “general principle”, and “presuppose”.</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p>Now certain words have specific meanings within the LSAT. All three of these words appear repeatedly in LR questions and we need to memorize them:</p>\\\\n\\\\n<div class=\\\\\\"definition\\\\\\"><strong>Presuppose:</strong> in daily usage, a presupposition can mean either an assumption or something that is necessary. On the LSAT presuppose simply means “require”. (It’s talking about a necessary condition, something that we will soon cover.)</div>\\\\n\\\\n<div class=\\\\\\"definition\\\\\\"><strong>Illustrate:</strong> an illustration, as it appears in a LR question, is simply referring to an example/examples</div>\\\\n\\\\n<div class=\\\\\\"definition\\\\\\"><strong>General Principle:</strong> we briefly touched upon the nature of principles on the LSAT in the last chapter, and will do so again further down the road. But for now, just know that a principle is a rule on what we should/shouldn’t do; and that it applies to multiple conditions and scenarios.</div>\\\\n\\\\n<p>For example, “<em>John is always tired in the morning, he should go to bed earlier</em>” is not a principle, we are just talking about one person here. “<em>Those who are tired in the morning should strive to get to bed earlier</em>” is a principle. It’s no longer just about John anymore, it is a recommendation for a certain category of people.</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p>So now there are three requirements that must be met if we were to select this hypothetical answer choice as the correct answer. First, the statement in question must be an example; second, behind this example is a principle on what we should or shouldn’t do; and lastly, this principle is required in order for the author to advance their argument.</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p>Take a look at this example:</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p class=\\\\\\"example\\\\\\"><span …2204 tokens truncated…strong> I’ve studied so hard for the finals, I’m definitely going to ace the exam!</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p class=\\\\\\"example\\\\\\"><strong>David:</strong> <span class=\\\\\\"underlined\\\\\\">Studying hard doesn’t guarantee you will do well.</span> The exams are graded on a curve, and everyone else has studied just as hard.</p>\\"\\r\\n217:    \\"breakdownHtml\\": \\"<p>There are two important pieces of information in this answer choice that can help us determine whether it’s the correct answer. First, it is “required” to establish the argument’s main conclusion. That means it’s necessary in order for the main conclusion to be valid. So there’s no way this answer choice is talking about the conclusion itself, it’s therefore either a premise or an intermediate conclusion.</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p>Second, the answer choice says it’s a claim for which no justification is provided. Remember what we said about how to differentiate between premises and intermediate conclusions in the previous chapter? Premises support the conclusion but do not have any other statements supporting them, whereas intermediate conclusions both support the conclusion and are supported by premises. So when we have a statement which supports the conclusion but is not supported by others, it can only mean one thing: we are talking about a premise here.</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p>So we are essentially talking about a premise that is required to establish the truth of the conclusion. In other words, if the premise was untrue, the conclusion would not stand. Take a look at the following example for more clarity:</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p class=\\\\\\"example\\\\\\">I found an Olympic gold medal in the swim team locker room. It must have originally belonged to John. John is always losing things and <span class=\\\\\\"underlined\\\\\\">he participated in the Olympics.</span></p>\\\\n\\\\n<p>John’s participation in the Olympics is a requirement for the example’s conclusion to stand. In order for John to be the original owner of the medal, he MUST have been a participant at least.</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p>John’s participation in the Olympics is also a premise that supports the example argument’s conclusion.</p>\\"\\r\\n240:      \\"Does the claim establish the main conclusion? With the source rule that all practicing lawyers passed the bar, John’s being a practicing lawyer establishes that he passed.\\",\\r\\n242:      \\"John’s law-firm position supports his being a practicing lawyer; that intermediate conclusion, together with the stated rule, establishes that he passed the bar.\\",\\r\\n245:    \\"breakdownHtml\\": \\"<p>Very similar to the example listed above, but with two important caveats. This answer choice HAS justification provided, while supporting the main conclusion, so it’s an intermediate conclusion. It also <strong>guarantees/establishes</strong> the truth of the conclusion.</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p class=\\\\\\"example\\\\\\">All practicing lawyers must have passed the bar. John is a partner at a prestigious law firm and therefore <span class=\\\\\\"underlined\\\\\\">he is a practicing lawyer</span>, so he must have passed the bar.</p>\\\\n\\\\n<p>John being a practicing lawyer is supported by the fact that he is a partner at a law firm. This satisfies the intermediate conclusion requirement. Does John being a practicing lawyer establish the fact that he has passed the bar? Yes, it is mentioned in the first statement/premise that all practicing lawyers must have passed the bar.</p>\\"\\r\\n345:      \\"Not the conclusion of the argument as a whole rules out the main conclusion. Intended describes what the support is meant to do.\\",\\r\\n416:    \\"meaning\\": \\"A rule about what should or shouldn\'t be done that covers many cases.\\",\\r\\n"}'}]

Centralised here so the same weights apply across\\r\\n// surfaces and the weights stay easy to tune.\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { LESSONS, DRILLS, FLASHCARDS } from \\"../data/mockData\\";\\r\\nimport { ANSWER_CHOICE_ITEMS } from \\"../data/answerChoiceDrill\\";\\r\\nimport { GAUNTLET_QUIZ_ITEMS } from \\"../data/abstractChoices\\";\\r\\nimport type { BootcampState } from \\"../types\\";\\r\\nimport { sourceExerciseIds } from \\"./grading-status\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface ProgressBreakdown {\\r\\n  lessons: { done: number; total: number; pct: number };\\r\\n  translator: { done: number; total: number; pct: number };\\r\\n  gauntlet: { done: number; total: number; pct: number };\\r\\n  drills: { done: number; total: number; pct: number };\\r\\n  flashcards: { done: number; total:  gauntlet: 0.2,\\r\\n  drills: 0.2,\\r\\n  flashcards: 0.15,\\r\\n  finalSet: 0.1,\\r\\n} as const;\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction pct(done: number, total: number): number {\\r\\n  if (total <= 0) return 0;\\r\\n  return Math.min(100, Math.round((done / total) * 100));\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function computeProgress(state: BootcampState): ProgressBreakdown {\\r\\n  const lessonsTotal = Set(ANSWER_CHOICE_ITEMS.map((i) => i.id));\\r\\n  const translatorTotal = translatorIds.size;\\r\\n  const translatorDone = [...sourceExerciseIds(state.drillAttempts)].filter((id) =>    state.gauntletAttempts.map((a) => a.choiceId).filter((id) => gauntletIds.has(id)),\\r\\n  ).size;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const drillsTotal = DRILLS.length;\\r\\n  const drillIds = new Set(DRILLS.map((item) => item.id));\\r\\n  const drillsDone = state.completedDrills.filter((id) => drillIds.has(id)).length;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const flashcardsTotal = FLASHCARDS.length;\\r\\n  const flashcardsDone = Object.values(state.cards).filter((c) => c.status === \\"mastered\\").length;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const finalSetTotal = 1;\\r\\n  const finalSetDone = state.finalSetScore ? 1 : 0;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const lessonsPct = pct(lessonsDone, lessonsTotal);\\r\\n  const translatorPct = pct(translatorDone, translatorTotal);\\r\\n  const gauntletPct = pct(gauntletDone, gauntletTotal);\\r\\n  const drillsPct = pct(drillsDone, drillsTotal);\\r\\n  const flashcardsPct = pct(flashcardsDone, flashcardsTotal);\\r\\n  const finalSetPct = pct(finalSetDone, finalSetTotal);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const overallPct = Math.round(\\r\\n    lessonsPct * WEIGHTS.lessons +\\r\\n      translatorPct * WEIGHTS.translator +\\r\\n      gauntletPct * WEIGHTS.gauntlet +\\r\\n      drillsPct * WEIGHTS.drills +\\r\\n      flashcardsPct * WEIGHTS.flashcards +\\r\\n      finalSetPct * WEIGHTS.finalSet,\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    lessons: { done: lessonsDone, total: lessonsTotal, pct: lessonsPct },\\r\\n    translator: { done: translatorDone, total: translatorTotal, pct: translatorPct },\\r\\n    gauntlet: { done: gauntletDone, total: gauntletTotal, pct: gauntletPct },\\r\\n    drills: { done: drillsDone, total: drillsTotal, pct: drillsPct },\\r\\n    flashcards: { done: flashcardsDone, total: flashcardsTotal, pct: flashcardsPct },\\r\\n    finalSet: { done: finalSetDone, total: finalSetTotal, pct: finalSetPct },\\r\\n    overallPct,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n// recommendNext: pick the surface with the lowest progress that still has\\r\\n// runway. Returns a tuple of (label, view-state, blurb) the dashboard can\\r\\n// render in the \\"Recommended next\\" tile.\\r\\nexport function recommendNext(p: ProgressBreakdown): {\\r\\n  label: string;\\r\\n  view: import(\\"../types\\").ViewState;\\r\\n  blurb: string;\\r\\n} {\\r\\n  const candidates: Array<{\\r\\n    key: keyof Omit<ProgressBreakdown, \\"overallPct\\">;\\r\\n    label: string;\\r\\n    view: import(\\"../types\\").ViewState;\\r\\n    blurb: string;\\r\\n  }> = [\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      key: \\"lessons\\",\\r\\n      label: \\"Guided lessons\\",\\r\\n      view: \\"lesson\\",\\r\\n      blurb: \\"Work through the next lesson to keep building the S + K method.\\",\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      key: \\"translator\\",\\r\\n      label: \\"Translator\\",\\r\\n      view: \\"lesson\\",\\r\\n      blurb: \\"Translate the next abstract question stem and see the walkthrough.\\",\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      key: \\"gauntlet\\",\\r\\n      label: \\"Decoding Gauntlet\\",\\r\\n      view: \\"lesson\\",\\r\\n      blurb: \\"Decode one more abstract answer choice and stress-test your translations.\\",\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      key: \\"drills\\",\\r\\n      label: \\"Targeted drills\\",\\r\\n      view: \\"drill\\",\\r\\n      blurb: \\"Pick a drill type and grind a few more reps under timed conditions.\\",\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      key: \\"flashcards\\",\\r\\n      label: \\"Flashcards\\",\\r\\n      view: \\"flashcards\\",\\r\\n      blurb: \\"Burn down the due-now queue before it builds up.\\",\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      key: \\"finalSet\\",\\r\\n      label: \\"Final timed set\\",\\r\\n      view: \\"timed_set\\",\\r\\n      blurb: \\"Run the timed simulation when the other surfaces are at 100%.\\",\\r\\n    },\\r\\n  ];\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Find the first candidate with pct < 100; if none, default to final set.\\r\\n  const next = candidates.find((c) => p[c.key].pct < 100) ?? candidates[candidates.length - 1];\\r\\n  return { label: next.label, view: next.view, blurb: next.blurb };\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

Students\\r\\n * cannot delete these rows (RLS: admin-only delete), so cloud copies are\\r\\n * never lost unless an admin explicitly removes them.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Semantics:\\r\\n *   - Writes: localStorage stays the fast local cache; the cloud write is\\r\\n *     debounced (~1s) and retried on failure. Also flushed on tab hide/close.\\r\\n *   - Removals: mirrored as a tombstone ({ v: null }) rather than a delete,\\r\\n *     so intentional resets don\'t get resurrected on the next hydrate.\\r\\n *   - Hydrate (on sign-in): pulls all \'localstore\' rows and fills ONLY keys\\r\\n *     missing locally. A fresh device gets its data back; an active device\\r\\n *     never has in-progress local state clobbered.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The raw localStorage string is stored verbatim (data = { v: string|null }),\\r\\n * so readers keep using their existing JSON.parse paths unchanged.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { supabase } from \'@/integrations/supabase/client\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst TABLE_NAME = \'localstore\';\\r\\nconst FLUSH_DEBOUNCE_MS = 1000;\\r\\n\\r\\nlet currentUserId: string | null = null;\\r\\nconst pending = new Map<string, string | null>(); // key -> raw value (null = tombstone)\\r\\nlet flushTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;\\r\\nlet lifecycleBound = false;\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction bindLifecycle() {\\r\\n  if (lifecycleBound || typeof window === \'undefined\') return;\\r\\n  lifecycleBound = true;\\r\\n  window.addEventListener(\'beforeunload\', () => void flush());\\r\\n  document.addEventListener(\'visibilitychange\', () => {\\r\\n    if (document.visibilityState === \'hidden\') void flush();\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction scheduleFlush() {\\r\\n  if (flushTimer) clearTimeout(flushTimer);\\r\\n  flushTimer = setTimeout(() => void flush(), FLUSH_DEBOUNCE_MS);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function flush(): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  if (!currentUserId || pending.size === 0) return;\\r\\n  const batch = Array.from(pending.entries());\\r\\n  pending.clear();\\r\\n  if (flushTimer) {\\r\\n    clearTimeout(flushTimer);\\r\\n    flushTimer = null;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const rows = batch.map(([key, value]) => ({\\r\\n      user_id: currentUserId!,\\r\\n      table_name: TABLE_NAME,\\r\\n      record_id: key,\\r\\n      data: { v: value },\\r\\n    }));\\r\\n    const { error } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\'bootcamp_kv\')\\r\\n      .upsert(rows, { onConflict: \'user_id,table_name,record_id\' });\\r\\n    if (error) {\\r\\n      console.warn(\'[cloudMirror] upsert failed:\', error.message);\\r\\n      for (const [k, v] of batch) pending.set(k, v);\\r\\n      scheduleFlush();\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.warn(\'[cloudMirror] flush threw\', e);\\r\\n    for (const [k, v] of batch) pending.set(k, v);\\r\\n    scheduleFlush();\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Pull cloud copies into localStorage for keys this device doesn\'t have. */\\r\\nasync function hydrate(userId: string): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const { data, error } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\'bootcamp_kv\')\\r\\n      .select(\'record_id, data\')\\r\\n      .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\\r\\n      .eq(\'table_name\', TABLE_NAME);\\r\\n    if (error) {\\r\\n      console.warn(\'[cloudMirror] hydrate failed:\', error.message);\\r\\n      return;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    let restored = 0;\\r\\n    for (const row of data ?? []) {\\r\\n      const value = (row.data as { v?: string | null } | null)?.v;\\r\\n      if (value == null) continue; // tombstone or empty\\r\\n      try {\\r\\n        if (localStorage.getItem(row.record_id) === null) {\\r\\n          localStorage.setItem(row.record_id, value);\\r\\n          restored += 1;\\r\\n        }\\r\\n      } catch {\\r\\n        /* storage unavailable */\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    if (restored > 0 && typeof window !== \'undefined\') {\\r\\n      window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent(\'cloud-mirror-hydrated\', { detail: { restored } }));\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.warn(\'[cloudMirror] hydrate threw\', e);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Start mirroring for a signed-in user and restore any missing local data. */\\r\\nexport function initCloudMirror(userId: string): void {\\r\\n  if (!userId || currentUserId === userId) return;\\r\\n  currentUserId = userId;\\r\\n  bindLifecycle();\\r\\n  void hydrate(userId);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Stop mirroring (sign-out). Pending writes are flushed first if possible. */\\r\\nexport function stopCloudMirror(): void {\\r\\n  void flush();\\r\\n  currentUserId = null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** localStorage.setItem + cloud mirror. Drop-in replacement. */\\r\\nexport function mirroredSetItem(key: string, value: string): void {\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    localStorage.setItem(key, value);\\r\\n  } catch {\\r\\n    /* storage unavailable */\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  if (currentUserId) {\\r\\n    pending.set(key, value);\\r\\n    scheduleFlush();\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** localStorage.removeItem + cloud tombstone (prevents hydrate resurrection). */\\r\\nexport function mirroredRemoveItem(key: string): void {\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    localStorage.removeItem(key);\\r\\n  } catch {\\r\\n    /* storage unavailable */\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  if (currentUserId) {\\r\\n    pending.set(key, null);\\r\\n    scheduleFlush();\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': </section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function AnswerChoiceDrill({ onDone }: { onDone: () => void }) {\\r\\n  const { bootcampState, loading, markTranslatorComplete, markDrillComplete, useState<SourceExerciseSession>(emptySourceSession);\\r\\n  const sessionRef = useRef(session);\\r\\n  const [ready, setReady] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const [saveError, setSaveError] = useState(\\"\\");\\r\\n  const [storageError, setStorageError] = useState(false);\\r\\n  const initialized = useRef(\\"\\");\\r\\n  const dirty = useRef(false);\\r\\n  const sectionRef = useRef<HTMLElement>(null);\\r\\n  const sourceRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);\\r\\n  const reviewedWrites = useRef(new Set<string>());\\r\\n  const pendingReviews = useRef(new Map<string, Promise<boolean>>());\\r\\n  const scopeRef = useRef(key);\\r\\n  scopeRef.current = key;\\r\\n\\r\\n  function commit(next: SourceExerciseSession) {\\r\\n    dirty.current = true;\\r\\n    sessionRef.current = next;\\r\\n    setSession(next);\\r\\n    setStorageError(!saveSourceSession(key, next));\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (loading || initialized.current === SOURCE_REVIEW_TAG).map((a) => a.drillId));\\r\\n    pendingReviews.current.clear();\\r\\n    const restored = restoreSourceSession(loadSourceSession(key), bootcampState.drillAttempts);\\r\\n    sessionRef.current = restored; setSession(restored); setReady(true); setBusy(false); setSaveError(\\"\\"); setStorageError(false);\\r\\n  }, [loading, key, bootcampState.drillAttempts]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    function onHydrated() {\\r\\n      if (dirty.current || !ready) return;\\r\\n      const saved = loadSourceSession(key);\\r\\n      if (saved) { const restored = restoreSourceSession(saved, bootcampState.drillAttempts); sessionRef.current = restored; setSession(restored); }\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    window.addEventListener(\\"cloud-mirror-hydrated\\", onHydrated);\\r\\n    return () => window.removeEventListener(\\"cloud-mirror-hydrated\\", onHydrated);\\r\\n  }, [key, ready, bootcampState.drillAttempts]);\\r\\n  useEffect(() => { sectionRef.current?.scrollIntoView({ block: \\"start\\" }); }, [session.index, session.finished]);\\r\\n  const item = ANSWER_CHOICE_ITEMS[session.index];\\r\\n  const draft = session.drafts[item.id] ?? { requirements: \\"\\", example: \\"\\", revealed: false };\\r\\n  const hasWrittenWork = Boolean(draft.requirements.trim() && draft.example.trim());\\r\\n\\r\\n  async function recordReview(): Promise<boolean> {\\r\\n    if (reviewedWrites.current.has(item.id)) return true;\\r\\n    const pending = pendingReviews.current.get(item.id);\\r\\n    if (pending) return pending;\\r\\n    const requestKey = key;\\r\\n    setBusy(true);\\r\\n    const request = (async () => {\\r\\n      try {\\r\\n        const result = await markDrillComplete(item.id, \\"translation\\", false, undefined, undefined, SOURCE_REVIEW_TAG);\\r\\n        if (scopeRef.current !== requestKey) return false;\\r\\n        if (!result.ok) { setSaveError(\\"Your review could not be saved to your account. Your place is kept on this browser. Try again.\\"); return false; }\\r\\n        reviewedWrites.current.add(item.id); setSaveError(\\"\\"); return true;\\r\\n      } catch { setSaveError(\\"Your review could not be saved. Your place is kept on this browser. Try again.\\"); return false; }\\r\\n      finally { pendingReviews.current.delete(item.id); if (scopeRef.current === requestKey) setBusy(false); }\\r\\n    })();\\r\\n    pendingReviews.current.set(item.id, request);\\r\\n    return request;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (!ready || session.finished || !draft.revealed || !sourceRef.current) return;\\r\\n    // This effect runs after the original breakdown has actually rendered.\\r\\n    if (!session.reviewedIds.includes(item.id)) commit({ ...sessionRef.current, reviewedIds: [...sessionRef.current.reviewedIds, item.id] });\\r\\n    void recordReview();\\r\\n  }, [ready, key, session.index, session.finished, draft.revealed]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  function updateDraft(field: \\"requirements\\" | \\"example\\", value: string) {\\r\\n    commit({ ...sessionRef.current, drafts: { ...sessionRef.current.drafts, [item.id]: { ...draft, [field]: value } } });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  function showBreakdown() {\\r\\n    commit({ ...sessionRef.current, drafts: { ...sessionRef.current.drafts, [item.id]: { ...draft, revealed: true } } });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  async function next() {\\r\\n    setSaveError(\\"\\");\\r\\n    if (!await recordReview()) return;\\r\\n    setBusy(true);\\r\\n    let current = sessionRef.current;\\r\\n    if (hasWrittenWork && !current.practicedIds.includes(item.id)) {\\r\\n      const work = `What must be present:\\\\n${draft.requirements.trim()}\\\\n\\\\nMy example argument and described statement:\\\\n${draft.example.trim()}`;\\r\\n      const translated = await markTranslatorComplete(item.stemNumber, [], work);\\r\\n      const recorded = translated.ok && await markDrillComplete(item.id, \\"translation\\", false, undefined, undefined, \\"source_exercise_ungraded\\");\\r\\n      if (!translated.ok || !recorded || !recorded.ok) { setSaveError(\\"Your written work could not be saved. Keep this page open and try again.\\"); setBusy(false); return; }\\r\\n      current = { ...sessionRef.current, practicedIds: [...sessionRef.current.practicedIds, item.id] };\\r\\n      commit(current);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    const last = session.index === ANSWER_CHOICE_ITEMS.length - 1;\\r\\n    if (last && current.practicedIds.length === ANSWER_CHOICE_ITEMS.length) {\\r\\n      const result = await markModuleDrillComplete(\\"m3\\");\\r\\n      if (!result.ok) { setSaveError(\\"Your practice completion could not be saved. Try again.\\"); setBusy(false); return; }\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    commit({ ...current, index: last ? session.index : session.index + 1, finished: last });\\r\\n    setBusy(false);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (loading || !ready || initialized.current !== key) return <p role=\\"status\\">Loading your saved practice...</p>;\\r\\n  if (session.finished) return <section ref={sectionRef} className=\\"source-review-summary space-y-5\\">\\r\\n    <h1 className=\\"text-3xl font-black\\">Your answer-choice review</h1>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"text-xl\\"><strong>{session.reviewedIds.length} of 15 breakdowns reviewed.</strong></p>\\r\\n    <p><strong>{session.practicedIds.length} of 15 written exercises saved.</strong> Reviewing a breakdown does not earn correctness or mastery.</p>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"choice-completion-note\\">Your place and drafts are saved on this browser.</p>\\r\\n    <button className=\\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper\\" onClick={() => commit({ ...sessionRef.current, index: 0, finished: false, drafts: Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(sessionRef.current.drafts).map(([id, value]) => [id, { ...value, revealed: false }])) })}>Review from the beginning</button>\\r\\n    <button className=\\"brutal-btn\\" onClick={() => session.practicedIds.length === 15 ? onDone() : setCurrentView(\\"dashboard\\")}>{session.practicedIds.length === 15 ? \\"Continue to the next module\\" : \\"Back to dashboard\\"}</button>\\r\\n  </section>;\\r\\n  return <section ref={sectionRef} className=\\"abstract-choice-exercise scroll-mt-24\\">\\r\\n    <header className=\\"choice-exercise-header\\">\\r\\n      <p className=\\"choice-exercise-counter\\">Answer choice {session.index + 1} of {ANSWER_CHOICE_ITEMS.length}</p>\\r\\n      <div role=\\"progressbar\\" aria-label=\\"Breakdowns reviewed\\" aria-valuemin={0} aria-valuemax={15} aria-valuenow={session.reviewedIds.length} className=\\"choice-exercise-progress\\"><div style={{ width: `${session.reviewedIds.length / 15 * 100}%` }} /></div>\\r\\n      <p className=\\"choice-exercise-status\\">{session.reviewedIds.length} reviewed / {session.practicedIds.length} written</p>\\r\\n    </header>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"choice-question-card\\">\\r\\n      <div className=\\"choice-question-eyebrow\\"><h1>What would this answer choice describe?</h1><span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">{String(session.index + 1).padStart(2, \\"0\\")}</span></div>\\r\\n      <p className=\\"choice-question-text\\" aria-label=\\"Answer choice\\">{item.stemText}</p>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    <ChoiceResponseTimeline disabled={busy || draft.revealed} steps={[\\r\\n      { id: \\"choice-requirements\\", label: \\"What must be present for this answer choice to be correct?\\", value: draft.requirements, rows: 4, onChange: (value) => updateDraft(\\"requirements\\", value) },\\r\\n      { id: \\"choice-example\\", label: \\"Write your own example argument. Identify the statement this answer choice describes.\\", value: draft.example, rows: 6, onChange: (value) => updateDraft(\\"example\\", value) },\\r\\n    ]} />\\r\\n    {!draft.revealed && <button onClick={showBreakdown} className=\\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper choice-reveal-button\\"><BookOpen className=\\"w-5 h-5\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\" />Show breakdown</button>}\\r\\n    {draft.revealed && <>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"choice-breakdown-card\\"><div className=\\"choice-breakdown-heading\\"><BookOpen aria-hidden=\\"true\\" /><h2>The breakdown</h2></div><div ref={sourceRef} data-testid=\\"source-breakdown\\" className=\\"choice-breakdown-prose\\" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: item.breakdownHtml }} /></div>\\r\\n      <p className=\\"choice-completion-note\\">{hasWrittenWork ? \\"Compare your work with the breakdown. Your responses are saved as practice, without a correctness score.\\" : \\"Review the breakdown, then continue. Reviewed breakdowns and written exercises are tracked separately.\\"}</p>\\r\\n      {saveError && <p role=\\"alert\\">{saveError}</p>}\\r\\n      <button className=\\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper choice-reveal-button\\" disabled={busy} onClick={next}>{busy ? \\"Saving...\\" : session.index === 14 ? \\"Finish the exercise\\" : \\"Next answer choice\\"}<ArrowRight className=\\"w-5 h-5\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\" /></button>\\r\\n    </>}\\r\\n    <p className=\\"choice-completion-note\\">{storageError ? \\"Drafts could not be saved on this browser. Keep this page open.\\" : \\"Your place and unfinished responses are saved on this browser.\\"}</p>\\r\\n  </section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 17.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"5392a5","wall_time_seconds":0.661923,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1991,"output":"import { useMemo } from \\"react\\";\\r\\nimport { useApp } from \\"../../store/AppContext\\";\\r\\nimport { LESSONS, FLASHCARDS } from \\"../../data/mockData\\";\\r\\nimport { ANSWER_CHOICE_ITEMS } from \\"../../data/answerChoiceDrill\\";\\r\\nimport { GAUNTLET_QUIZ_ITEMS } from \\"../../data/abstractChoices\\";\\r\\nimport { isUngradedAttempt, sourceExerciseIds } from \\"../../lib/grading-status\\";\\r\\nimport { CheckCircle2, ArrowRight, BookOpen, Target, Brain, Flag, Timer, Layers, FileText } from \\"lucide-react\\";\\r\\nimport { motion, useReducedMotion } from \\"framer-motion\\";\\r\\nimport { CardReview, ConfidenceLevel, DrillAttempt, DrillType } from \\"../../types\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nconst TRAP_LABEL: Record<string, string> = {\\r\\n  wrong_role: \\"Wrong role attribution\\",\\r\\n  wrong_method: \\"Wrong method of reasoning\\",\\r\\n  structural_mismatch: \\"Structural mismatch (necessary vs sufficient, scope shift)\\",\\r\\n  attractive_language: \\"One-word trap (extreme language, scope creep)\\",\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nconst TYPE_LABEL: Record<DrillType, string> = {\\r\\n  role: \\"Role\\",\\r\\n  method: \\"Method\\",\\r\\n  flaw: \\"Flaw\\",\\r\\n  one_word_trap: \\"One-word trap\\",\\r\\n  keyword_extraction: \\"Keyword extraction\\",\\r\\n  term_definition: \\"Term definition\\",\\r\\n  translation: \\"Translation\\",\\r\\n  matching: \\"Matching\\",\\r\\n  mixed: \\"Mixed\\",\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n// Compute the most-fallen-into trap from the student\'s wrong drill attempts.\\r\\n// Returns null when there isn\'t enough data to make an honest claim.\\r\\nfunction pickWeakestTrap(attempts: DrillAttempt[]): { tag: string; count: number } | null {\\r\\n  const wrong = attempts.filter((a) => !a.correct && a.trapTag && !isUngradedAttempt(a.trapTag));\\r\\n  if (wrong.length < 2) return null;\\r\\n  const counts = new Map<string, number>();\\r\\n  for (const a of wrong) {\\r\\n    if (!a.trapTag) continue;\\r\\n    counts.set(a.trapTag, (counts.get(a.trapTag) ?? 0) + 1);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  let top: { tag: string; count: number } | null = null;\\r\\n  for (const [tag, count] of counts) {\\r\\n    if (!top || count > top.count) top = { tag, count };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return top;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n// Compute the drill type with the highest hit rate among ≥3 attempts.\\r\\nfunction pickStrongestType(\\r\\n  stats: Record<string, { correct: number; total: number } | undefined>,\\r\\n): { type: DrillType; rate: number; total: number } | null {\\r\\n  let best: { type: DrillType; rate: number; total: number } | null = null;\\r\\n  for (const [type, s] of Object.entries(stats)) {\\r\\n    if (!s || s.total < 3) continue;\\r\\n    const rate = s.correct / s.total;\\r\\n    if (!best || rate > best.rate) {\\r\\n      best = { type: type as DrillType, rate, total: s.total };\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return best;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function CompletionReport() {\\r\\n  const { bootcampState, setCurrentView } = useApp();\\r\\n  const prefersReducedMotion = useReducedMotion();\\r\\n\\r\\n  const lessonsCompleted = bootcampState.completedLessons.length;\\r\\n  const drillsCompleted = bootcampState.completedDrills.length;\\r\\n  const cardsReviewed = Object.values(bootcampState.cards as Record<string, CardReview>)\\r\\n    .filter((c) => c.status !== \\"unseen\\").length;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const totalCorrect = Object.values(bootcampState.drillsStats).reduce(\\r\\n    (acc, curr) => acc + (curr?.correct ?? 0),\\r\\n    0,\\r\\n  );\\r\\n  const totalAttempted = Object.values(bootcampState.drillsStats).reduce(\\r\\n    (acc, curr) => acc + (curr?.total ?? 0),\\r\\n    0,\\r\\n  );\\r\\n  const accuracy = totalAttempted > 0 ? Math.round((totalCorrect / totalAttempted) * 100) : null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const weakest = pickWeakestTrap(bootcampState.drillAttempts);\\r\\n  const strongest = pickStrongestType(bootcampState.drillsStats);\\r\\n  const hasData = drillsCompleted > 0 || lessonsCompleted > 0 || cardsReviewed > 0;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const totalTimeMs = useMemo(\\r\\n    () => bootcampState.drillAttempts.reduce((sum, a) => sum + (a.timeMs ?? 0), 0),\\r\\n    [bootcampState.drillAttempts],\\r\\n  );\\r\\n  const totalTimeMinutes = Math.round(totalTimeMs / 60_000);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const perTypeBreakdown = useMemo(() => {\\r\\n    const out: { type: DrillType; correct: number; total: number; pct: number }[] = [];\\r\\n    for (const [type, stats] of Object.entries(bootcampState.drillsStats)) {\\r\\n      if (!stats || stats.total === 0) continue;\\r\\n      out.push({\\r\\n        type: type as DrillType,\\r\\n        correct: stats.correct,\\r\\n        total: stats.total,\\r\\n        pct: Math.round((stats.correct / stats.total) * 100),\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    return out.sort((a, b) => b.total - a.total);\\r\\n  }, [bootcampState.drillsStats]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const confidenceCalibration = useMemo(() => {\\r\\n    const buckets: Record<ConfidenceLevel, { correct: number; total: number }> = {\\r\\n      Guessing: { correct: 0, total: 0 },\\r\\n      Unsure: { correct: 0, total: 0 },\\r\\n      \\"Pretty sure\\": { correct: 0, total: 0 },\\r\\n      Confident: { correct: 0, total: 0 },\\r\\n    };\\r\\n    for (const a of bootcampState.drillAttempts) {\\r\\n      if (isUngradedAttempt(a.trapTag)) continue;\\r\\n      const b = buckets[a.confidence];\\r\\n      if (!b) continue;\\r\\n      b.total += 1;\\r\\n      if (a.correct) b.correct += 1;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    return buckets;\\r\\n  }, [bootcampState.drillAttempts]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const trapFrequency = useMemo(() => {\\r\\n    const counts = new Map<string, number>();\\r\\n    for (const a of bootcampState.drillAttempts) {\\r\\n      if (a.correct || !a.trapTag || isUngradedAttempt(a.trapTag)) continue;\\r\\n      counts.set(a.trapTag, (counts.get(a.trapTag) ?? requestKey) return;\\r\\n      const recorded = translated.ok && await markDrillComplete(item.id, \\"translation\\", false, undefined, undefined, \\"source_exercise_ungraded\\");\\r\\n      if (scopeRef.current !== requestKey) return;\\r\\n      if (!translated.ok || !recorded || !recorded.ok) { setSaveError(\\"Your written work could not be saved. Keep this page open and try again.\\"); setBusy(false); return; }\\r\\n      current = { ...sessionRef.current, practicedIds: [...sessionRef.current.practicedIds, item.id] };\\r\\n      commit(current);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    const last = session.index === ANSWER_CHOICE_ITEMS.length - 1;\\r\\n    if (last && current.practicedIds.length === ANSWER_CHOICE_ITEMS.length) {\\r\\n      const result = await markModuleDrillComplete(\\"m3\\");\\r\\n      if (scopeRef.current !== requestKey) return;\\r\\n      if (!result.ok) { setSaveError(\\"Your practice completion could not be saved. Try again.\\"); setBusy(false); return; }\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    commit({ ...current, index: last ? session.index : session.index + 1, finished: last });\\r\\n    setBusy(false);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (loading || progressOwnerId !== (user?.id ?? null) || !ready || initialized.current !== key) return <p role=\\"status\\">Loading your saved practice...</p>;\\r\\n  if (session.finished) return <section ref={sectionRef} className=\\"source-review-summary space-y-5\\">\\r\\n    <h1 className=\\"text-3xl font-black\\">Your answer-choice review</h1>\\r\\n    <p className=\\"text-xl\\"><strong>{session.reviewedIds.length} of 15 breakdowns reviewed.</strong></p>\\r\\n    <p><strong>{session.practicedIds.length} of 15 written exercises saved.</strong> Reviewing a breakdown does not earn correctness or mastery.</p>\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 17.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b44005","wall_time_seconds":1.1693736000000001,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4277,"output":"# Current lecture copy ready for local review\\r\\n\\r\\nDate: 2026-10-13. **Draft connective teaching for Joshua review.** This is the current builder-ready proposal. It supersedes the guided choice-1 proposal and conditional lecture ending farther below. The lesson uses source choice 2 because its example shows the example-to-premise-to-conclusion relationship directly. No new scenarios are invented.\\r\\n\\r\\nPlacement: keep the Desktop source’s four introduction paragraphs exactly; follow them with the opening and guided example below. Keep the fifteen original choices/breakdowns exact and in order. Place the closing after the fifteenth original breakdown. No additional required modules are proposed. Source introduction/breakdown HTML remains untouched.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Draft opening and guided source example\\r\\n\\r\\n### An answer choice describes a relationship\\r\\n\\r\\nAn abstract answer choice tells us what a statement does in an argument. To understand it, we need more than the name of a role. We need to see the relationship the choice describes.\\r\\n\\r\\nA premise is a reason for accepting a conclusion. An example can illustrate that premise. Those are related jobs, but they are different jobs. The answer choice can ask us to follow both.\\r\\n\\r\\nConsider this original choice:\\r\\n\\r\\n> It is an illustration of a premise that is used to support the argument’s conclusion\\r\\n\\r\\n**Illustration** means an example. **Premise** tells us that the idea being illustrated is a reason the author uses. **Support the argument’s conclusion** tells us what that reason is used for.\\r\\n\\r\\nSo there are two relationships to check: the statement must be an example of the premise, and the premise must support the conclusion. Calling the statement “an example” gets us started. It does not finish the work.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Follow the original example\\r\\n\\r\\nThe following is the original source example, unchanged. The underlined statement is the one the choice describes:\\r\\n\\r\\n<p class=\\"example\\"><span class=\\"underlined\\">John has been smoking two packs a day since he was sixteen, last week he was diagnosed with lung cancer.</span> Smoking is a direct cause of lung cancer. We must avoid cigarettes at all costs if we want to stay healthy.</p>\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst, find the point the author wants us to accept. Here, the author says we must avoid cigarettes if we want to stay healthy.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhat reason does the author give? **Smoking is a direct cause of lung cancer.** That is the premise used to support the recommendation.\\r\\n\\r\\nNow look at the underlined statement. John smoked two packs a day and was diagnosed with lung cancer. The source presents John’s case as an example of the causal relationship stated in the premise.\\r\\n\\r\\nWe can now follow the whole description. **John’s case illustrates the premise. The premise supports the conclusion.** The choice describes what the underlined statement does, including the connection to the reason and the reason’s connection to the final point.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf we only noticed that the paragraph discusses smoking, we would miss the structure. If we only noticed that John’s case is an example, we would leave part of the choice unchecked. We need to know what it is an example of and how that idea is used in the argument.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis explains the source example’s role. It does not ask us to decide whether the argument has proved its causal claim. The task here is to understand the description and identify the relationship the source is showing.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Make each choice concrete\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each of the fifteen choices, there are two pieces of written work you can use to test your understanding.\\r\\n\\r\\n**State the requirements.** What would have to appear in an argument for the choice to describe it correctly? Keep every part of the description. A claim receiving support is different from a claim receiving no justification. A claim being required is different from a claim establishing the conclusion.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Create an example argument.** Write reasons and a conclusion, then identify the particular statement the choice would describe. Check where each requirement appears in your example. If one part is missing, you know what still needs work.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe original breakdown is available whenever you need it. You can try the written work first, open the explanation to learn the relationship, or return to revise your example after reading. Reading the breakdown and completing the written work are recorded separately.\\r\\n\\r\\nAs you continue, keep comparing the wording of the choice with what the statement actually does. The explanation should make the language more concrete. Your example gives you a chance to use that meaning yourself.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Draft closing after the fifteenth exact breakdown\\r\\n\\r\\n### Check the whole description\\r\\n\\r\\nThe fifteen choices use different words, but the task stays the same: turn the description into requirements you can check.\\r\\n\\r\\nStart with the idea the choice names. Is it an example, a principle, an observation, a hypothesis, or a conclusion? Then look at what happens to that idea. Is it used as support, challenged, denied, or offered as an explanation?\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep the strength of the wording. **Required** and **establishes** do different work. **Attempts** does not tell us that the attempt succeeds. **Explicitly stated** asks for something the argument actually says. Leaving out one of these details changes the description you are trying to satisfy.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe final choices also show why we trace each link of support. A statement can receive support and then support the main conclusion. Or it can be the main conclusion, supported by another statement that itself receives support. Ask why we should believe each claim and look for the reason the argument gives. Then ask what that claim supports in turn.\\r\\n\\r\\nYou can return to any choice and check your own example against its original breakdown. Have you included every required part? Have you identified the statement the choice describes? Does that statement actually do what the full choice says?\\r\\n\\r\\nThat completes this lecture. When you later meet this language in a full LSAT question, look for those same parts and relationships in the argument you are given.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Source map for this ready copy\\r\\n\\r\\n- Desktop `role_question_answer_choices_complete_transcription.html`: exact choice 2 and its complete example paragraph; breakdown 2 identifies smoking/causation as the premise and avoiding cigarettes as the conclusion. This draft adds reasoning connections without changing that example or the original breakdown.\\r\\n- Desktop choices/breakdowns 8–15: unsupported versus supported claim, required versus establishes, explicit conclusion, and support chains. The closing’s distinctions come from these original sections.\\r\\n- [Role recap drill](../corpus/Curriculum/Role/Homework/_scan_Role_Questions_Recap_Drill.md), Level 4: asks why we should believe a claim and whether the argument supplies a supporting statement. Its overbroad “only conclusion” claim is not adopted.\\r\\n- [Level 1 Answer Parsing and Mapping](../corpus/Curriculum/Abstract/Homework/Level%201%20Answer%20Parsing%20and%20Mapping.md), Part III: reverse-engineer requirements and create an example. No extra worksheet questions are imported.\\r\\n- [Advanced Parsing Answer Key](../corpus/Curriculum/Abstract/Homework/Advanced%20Parsing%20Answer%20Key.md), supported premise: a claim receives evidence and supports the main conclusion. Its separate methods/flaw material is outside this lecture.\\r\\n- [Teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md), [selected teaching standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md), [copy corrections](../wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md): plain meaning before terminology, visible reasoning, familiar source example, complete qualifications. Original supplied wording remains exact.\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything outside the quoted choice and original example is newly authored draft language. No content approval, app edit, commit, publishing, deployment or user memory update occurred in this drafting task.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Current source direction supersedes the draft below\\r\\n\\r\\nDate: 2026-10-13. The newest authoritative source is Joshua’s Desktop complete transcription. The active exercise shows each answer choice, asks the learner to state its requirements and create their own matching example, then immediately reveals the **exact original source breakdown**. All source wording, chapter/prior-material references, paragraph structure, bold, italics, and underlining are preserved. The four introduction paragraphs are also exact source HTML. The earlier polished breakdowns, authored seven-step walkthroughs, and 45-term bank below are historical draft work, superseded for the active exercise, not approved or shown as the current teaching flow.\\r\\n\\r\\nSource: `C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\Desktop\\\\role_question_answer_choices_complete_transcription.html`. SHA-256: `c914b12b0d61ba8e743e5e55979e48772d3147edcbc2b3dc305648fb3492ba61`.\\r\\n\\r\\nExact-source fixture: `src/topics/abstract/data/__fixtures__/role_question_answer_choices_complete_transcription.html`. Fidelity test: `src/topics/abstract/data/answerChoiceDrill.test.ts`. Neither source nor fixture is rewritten. No commit or deployment is authorized by this change.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Abstract answer-choice draft for Joshua review\\r\\n\\r\\nDate: 2026-10-13. Status: LOCAL DRAFT, not approved for publication.\\r\\n\\r\\nAll 15 answer choices are exact source text. All seven procedure steps preserve source wording and emphasis. Applied walkthroughs, ideal meanings, grading requirements, and new word-bank definitions are drafts. Existing Module 3 and Module 7 four-step routines and Module 7 repeated choices are retained per Joshua’s 2026-10-13 reply.\\r\\n\\r\\nSources:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\Downloads\\\\role_question_answer_choices_exercise_format.html`; SHA-256 `36dfa67be8874d5241837529553a0bd36302bfc806c8b8ec8baba247e88b6dde`.\\r\\n- `C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\Downloads\\\\answer_choice_traps_formatted.html`; SHA-256 `5a77739ab8d409d121181e00dd4f7af9943261001dc99d3bb27f22f8eb1bb827`.\\r\\n- Approved Module 2 definitions: `src/topics/abstract/components/views/AbstractNotes.tsx`, STATEMENT_WORDS and ACTION_WORDS. Approved below means inherited source/Module 2 wording, not new approval of this artifact.\\r\\n\\r\\nEditorial scope: remove chapter references and references to earlier exercises/material. Source examples and substantive claims remain. Source example 8 assumes Olympic participation is required for original ownership of the medal; retained as authored, not strengthened into a universal fact. Source explanations of hypothesis/explanation use the course’s causal sense. No new practice scenarios were invented.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Definitions needing approval\\r\\n\\r\\n- **support**: Give a reason to accept a claim.\\r\\n- **conclusion**: A claim the author supports with reasons.\\r\\n- **counter**: Answer or oppose a point against the argument.\\r\\n- **consideration**: A point that could count against the conclusion in this choice.\\r\\n- **might**: Could; the choice allows a possible objection.\\r\\n- **observation**: Something noticed or reported about the world.\\r\\n- **insufficient**: Not enough to establish the claim.\\r\\n- **justify**: Provide enough reason to accept a claim.\\r\\n- **claim**: A statement put forward as true.\\r\\n- **false**: Untrue.\\r\\n- **phenomenon**: The event, effect, or situation being explained.\\r\\n- **validity**: Whether the principle is true or acceptable in this choice.\\r\\n- **questions**: Challenges or calls into doubt.\\r\\n- **denies**: Says a claim is not true.\\r\\n- **assumed**: Taken for granted without being established.\\r\\n- **reasoning**: The way reasons are used to support a claim.\\r\\n- **rejects**: Refuses to accept.\\r\\n- **no justification**: No supporting reason for this claim is supplied in the argument.\\r\\n- **required**: Necessary for this argument; meeting it may not be enough.\\r\\n- **establish**: Secure the conclusion, rather than merely make it more likely.\\r\\n- **justification**: A reason supplied to support a claim.\\r\\n- **if true**: The stated result follows on the condition that the claim is true.\\r\\n- **establishes**: Secures the conclusion, rather than merely makes it more likely.\\r\\n- **purports**: Claims or attempts to do something, without saying it succeeds.\\r\\n- **attempts**: Tries to; success is not asserted.\\r\\n- **sufficiency**: Whether something is enough.\\r\\n- **evidence**: Information offered as support for a claim.\\r\\n- **as a whole**: Considering the complete argument and its final point.\\r\\n- **not the only**: There is at least one other.\\r\\n- **explicitly stated**: Said directly in the argument, rather than left unstated.\\r\\n- **intended**: Meant to do something.\\r\\n- **not the conclusion**: Does not function as the final point of the complete argument.\\r\\n- **provided**: Given within the argument.\\r\\n- **supported**: Given reasons or evidence to accept it.\\r\\n- **offered**: Presented for the stated purpose.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Inherited definitions\\r\\n\\r\\n- **illustrate**: Give an example.\\r\\n- **general principle**: A rule about what should or shouldn\'t be done that covers many cases.\\r\\n- **presupposes**: Require.\\r\\n- **illustration**: An example.\\r\\n- **premise**: A reason the author gives to support a conclusion.\\r\\n- **undermine**: Weaken. Push against.\\r\\n- **explanation**: Give the cause.\\r\\n- **main conclusion**: The final point the author wants you to accept.\\r\\n- **explain**: Give the cause.\\r\\n- **hypothesis**: A possible explanation: a proposed cause.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Choice 1\\r\\n\\r\\nSource exercise 1; walkthrough and meaning pending approval.\\r\\n\\r\\n> It is used to illustrate the general principle that the argument presupposes\\r\\n\\r\\n**Plain English:** The statement gives an example of a general rule that the argument requires.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Grading requirements (draft):**\\r\\n\\r\\n- The statement is an example.\\r\\n- The example illustrates a general principle.\\r\\n- The argument requires that principle.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Pull out “illustrate”, “general principle”, “presupposes”.\\r\\n2. The statement gives an example of a general rule that the argument requires.\\r\\n3. The statement must be an example, and the rule behind it must apply beyond that one case. John’s unhappiness despite his income is the source example.\\r\\n4. Does the statement illustrate the rule? Does the argument require that rule? John’s situation illustrates why money alone does not bring happiness.\\r\\n5. General means the principle covers multiple cases. Presupposes means required, not merely helpful.\\r\\n6. The example illustrates the principle that money alone is not enough for happiness; that principle is needed for the argument that we must pursue intangible things.\\r\\n7. Go back and forth between the choice and the argument. Check every requirement before accepting the choice.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Polished source breakdown:**\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>Whenever we are faced with an answer choice that looks super abstract, the first thing to do is to always <strong>extract what keywords we can</strong> to try to make sense of the whole thing. Three words stand out in this answer choice: “illustrate”, “general principle”, and “presuppose”.</p>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>Now certain words have specific meanings within the LSAT. All three of these words appear repeatedly in LR questions and we need to memorize them:</p>\\r\\n\\r\\n<div class=\\"definition\\"><strong>Presuppose:</strong> in daily usage, a presupposition can mean either an assumption or something that is necessary. On the LSAT presuppose simply means “require”. It is talking about a necessary condition.</div>\\r\\n\\r\\n<div class=\\"definition\\"><strong>Illustrate:</strong> an illustration, as it appears in a LR question, is simply referring to an example/examples</div>\\r\\n\\r\\n<div class=\\"definition\\"><strong>General Principle:</strong>  a principle is a rule on what we should/shouldn’t do; and that it applies to multiple conditions and scenarios.</div>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>For example, “<em>John is always tired in the morning, he should go to bed earlier</em>” is not a principle, we are just talking about one person here. “<em>Those who are tired in the morning should strive to get to bed earlier</em>” is a principle. It’s no longer just about John anymore, it is a recommendation for a certain category of people.</p>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>So now there are three requirements that must be met if we were to select this hypothetical answer choice as the correct answer. First, the statement in question must be an example; second, behind this example is a principle on what we should or shouldn’t do; and lastly, this principle is required in order for the author to advance their argument.</p>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>Take a look at this example:</p>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p class=\\"example\\"><span class=\\"underlined\\">Despite a high paying job, John was extremely stressed out and depressed</span>, so there are intangible things in life that we must pursue to find true happiness.</p>\\r\\n\\r\\n<p>John’s situation is the illustration of a general principle; namely that having a lot of money isn’t enough to make you happy. Is this principle required in order for the conclusion to stand? Yes, the conclusion says factors other than money must be considered in order to be happy. The underlined statement satisfies all three requirements and if this was a Role question, this would be the correct answer.</p>\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"055ecb","wall_time_seconds":0.6196481,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1954,"output":"import { useState } from \\"react\\";\\r\\nimport { Check, X } from \\"lucide-react\\";\\r\\nimport { cn } from \\"../../lib/utils\\";\\r\\nimport { ROLE_CHOICE_INTRO_HTML } from \\"../../data/answerChoiceDrill\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n// The seven module notes for the Abstract bootcamp, one component per note.\\r\\n// LessonReader maps lesson ids to these. Copy follows Joshua\'s teaching voice:\\r\\n// say what the thing is first, show it on one example, then let the student\\r\\n// do it. Modules 1 and 3 render the supplied Role introduction verbatim.\\r\\n\\r\\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- shared bits\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Panel({ children, tone = \\"ink\\" }: { children: React.ReactNode; tone?: \\"ink\\" | \\"yellow\\" }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div\\r\\n      className={cn(\\r\\n        \\"border-4 border-brutal-ink p-6 md:p-7 my-8 brutal-shadow\\",\\r\\n        tone === \\"ink\\" ? \\"bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper\\" : \\"bg-brutal-yellow text-brutal-ink\\",\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    >\\r\\n      <div className=\\"text-lg md:text-xl font-bold leading-snug\\">{children}</div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Label({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <span className=\\"block text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-brutal-ink-soft mb-2\\">\\r\\n      {children}\\r\\n    </span>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction AnswerChoice({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div className=\\"bg-white border-4 border-brutal-ink p-5 md:p-6 my-4 brutal-shadow-sm\\">\\r\\n      <Label>Answer choice</Label>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"text-lg md:text-xl font-semibold leading-snug\\">{children}</div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction RightWrongCard({ ok, title, children }: { ok: boolean; title: string; children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div\\r\\n      className={cn(\\r\\n        \\"border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 flex gap-3 items-start\\",\\r\\n        ok ? \\"bg-brutal-green/30\\" : \\"bg-brutal-red/10\\",\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    >\\r\\n      <span\\r\\n        className={cn(\\r\\n          \\"shrink-0 inline-flex items-center gap-1 border-2 border-brutal-ink px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-wider\\",\\r\\n          ok ? \\"bg-brutal-green\\" : \\"bg-brutal-red text-brutal-paper\\",\\r\\n        )}\\r\\n      >\\r\\n        {ok ? <Check className=\\"w-3.5 h-3.5\\" aria-hidden /> : <X className=\\"w-3.5 h-3.5\\" aria-hidden />}\\r\\n        {ok ? \\"Right\\" : \\"Wrong\\"}\\r\\n      </span>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"text-base leading-relaxed\\">\\r\\n        <span className=\\"font-bold\\">{title}</span> {children}\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 1\\r\\n\\r\\nconst ANSWER_CHOICE_TRAPS_HTML = \\"<h1>Answer Choice Traps</h1>\\\\n\\\\n<section class=\\\\\\"section\\\\\\">\\\\n  <div class=\\\\\\"section-number\\\\\\">Answer Choice Type 1</div>\\\\n  <h2>Half right half wrong answers</h2>\\\\n\\\\n  <p><strong>An essential habit to have for the hardest LR questions (and RC questions) is to carefully parse the answer choices, looking for suspicious keywords or concepts and connections that seem out of place.</strong></p>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>The test makers love to offer us an answer choice that is only partially correct. The first half of the answer may be correct, but the second half will contain out of scope information, for instance. So it’s crucial to review each answer word by word, looking for red flags.</p>\\\\n</section>\\\\n\\\\n<section class=\\\\\\"section\\\\\\">\\\\n  <div class=\\\\\\"section-number\\\\\\">Answer Choice Type 2</div>\\\\n  <h2>Answers describing something that did not happen</h2>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>For questions whose correct answer describes a feature of the stimuli’s argument (Role, Method, Flaw), we need to keep a watchful eye for answer choices describing something that does not happen in the argument.</p>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>In fact, identifying answer choices that deviate from the stimulus is a sure-fire way to eliminate erroneous contenders from the selection. For these four types of questions, read the answer choice, think about what it means, <span class=\\\\\\"underline\\\\\\">isolate any suspicious words in them</span>, and ask yourself, “does the author do this in their argument?”</p>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>But of course, this depends on the question type we are working on. Similar to Out of Scope answer choices, answer choices describing something that does not occur in the stimulus can work as correct Strengthen/Weaken answers, among others.</p>\\\\n</section>\\\\n\\\\n<section class=\\\\\\"section\\\\\\">\\\\n  <div class=\\\\\\"section-number\\\\\\">Answer Choice Type 3</div>\\\\n  <h2>Answer choices that are too vague/abstract</h2>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>Finally, we have seen how the correct answer can be vaguely worded. We have even practiced the art of extrapolating what an answer is referring to by analyzing its keywords.</p>\\\\n\\\\n  <p>Extracting certain keywords from vague or abstract answer choices in order to understand what that answer choice is actually talking about is also an invaluable skill. Unfortunately, it’s one of the most under-developed skills and often overlooked when students pursue speed at the expense of accuracy.</p>\\\\n\\\\n  \\\\n\\\\n  <p class=\\\\\\"final\\\\\\">Lastly, a common trick employed by the authors of the test is to have an almost perfect answer with a glaring error, usually due to one single word placed right next to the correct answer, which is worded vaguely. This is a trap that we shall see over and over again throughout the book.</p>\\\\n</section>\\";\\r\\nconst SOURCE_NOTE_CLASSES = \\"space-y-5 leading-relaxed [&_p]:my-5 [&_h1]:text-3xl [&_h1]:font-black [&_h1]:mb-6 [&_h2]:text-xl [&_h2]:font-bold [&_h2]:mt-6 [&_.section]:mt-8 [&_.section-number]:font-bold [&_.underline]:underline [&_.final]:font-semibold [&_.section:last-child]:border-2 [&_.section:last-child]:border-brutal-ink [&_.section:last-child]:bg-brutal-yellow/30 [&_.section:last-child]:p-5\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function WhyHardNote() {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div>\\r\\n      <div className={SOURCE_NOTE_CLASSES} dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: ROLE_CHOICE_INTRO_HTML }} />\\r\\n      <div className={SOURCE_NOTE_CLASSES} dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: ANSWER_CHOICE_TRAPS_HTML }} />\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 2\\r\\n\\r\\nconst STATEMENT_WORDS: { term: string; meaning: string; note?: string }[] = [\\r\\n  { term: \\"Premise\\", meaning: \\"A reason the author gives to support a conclusion.\\" },\\r\\n  { term: \\"Main conclusion\\", meaning: \\"The final point the author wants you to accept.\\" },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    term: \\"Intermediate conclusion\\",\\r\\n    meaning: \\"A claim that gets support and then gives support to the main conclusion.\\",\\r\\n    note: \\"Also called a supported premise.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  { term: \\"Fact\\", meaning: \\"A statement about the world, taken as true.\\", note: \\"\\\\\\"Water boils at 100 degrees Celsius at sea level.\\\\\\"\\" },\\r\\n  { term: \\"Decision\\", meaning: \\"A choice someone made or will make.\\", note: \\"\\\\\\"I\'m taking the train instead of the bus.\\\\\\"\\" },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    term: \\"Principle (general principle)\\",\\r\\n    meaning: \\"A rule about what should or shouldn\'t be done that covers many cases.\\",\\r\\n    note: \\"\\\\\\"People who are tired every morning should go to bed earlier\\\\\\" is a principle. \\\\\\"John is tired every morning, so he should go to bed earlier\\\\\\" is not. It\'s about one person.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    term: \\"Generalization\\",\\r\\n    meaning: \\"A broad statement about what is.\\",\\r\\n    note: \\"\\\\\\"All swans are white.\\\\\\" A principle says what should be. A generalization says what is.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    term: \\"Hypothesis\\",\\r\\n    meaning: \\"A possible explanation: a proposed cause.\\",\\r\\n    note: \\"Not just any claim. A hypothesis is a guess about why something happened.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\nconst ACTION_WORDS: { term: string; meaning: string; note?: string }[] = [\\r\\n  { term: \\"Illustrate\\", meaning: \\"Give an example.\\", note: \\"\\\\\\"It illustrates a principle\\\\\\" means it\'s an example of the principle.\\" },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    term: \\"Presuppose\\",\\r\\n    meaning: \\"Require.\\",\\r\\n    note: \\"If the argument presupposes something, the argument falls apart without it.\\",\\r\\n"}'}]
